_source line 1 · 2026-05-13T09:01:02.486Z_

You are Reviewer 1 (R1) for a top ML venue peer review. Your focus: **Methods and Experiments**. Use a 6-point scale (1=Strong Reject, 2=Reject, 3=Weak Reject, 4=Weak Accept, 5=Accept, 6=Strong Accept). Target acceptance rate ~25%.

## Paper Summary

Title: "What Controls the Discriminability of Recursive Synthetic Text? An Empirical Analysis via Data Processing Inequality"

The paper studies recursive synthetic text — text generated by models trained/prompted on previous synthetic outputs. It formalizes recursive generation as an iterated lossy channel, invokes DPI to prove a finite discrimination boundary K* must exist, then empirically measures K* across configurations.

**Central claim**: K* is configuration-dependent (ranges 1–3), not a universal constant. Factors: decoding strategy, model scale, recursion mechanism, feature representation.

**Key results**:
- Decoding: greedy→K*=1, nucleus p=0.95→K*=2, T=1.2→K*=3
- Scale: 1.5B→K*=2, 7B→K*=1
- Recursion: prompt-chain→K*=2, retrain-chain(LoRA)→K*=1
- Features: hand-crafted 15D→K*=2, learned embeddings+MLP→K*=3
- Graduated filtering vs binary: Δ<0.5pp, p>0.10 (negative result)
- Base vs Instruct: both K*=2, base slightly higher 1v2 AUC
- Seed variation: K*=2 for baseline Qwen-cont is fragile (2/3 seeds fail CI criterion)
- Binary detectors (DivEye, Binoculars): near-chance at depth ordering (1v2 AUC 0.53–0.55)
- LLM zero-shot: near-random ordinal accuracy (0.23–0.25)
- Naturalistic validation on Common Crawl: moderate correlations (ρ=0.28–0.31)
- Dose-response: graduated advantage never reaches significance even at 50% injection

**Method details**:
- Depth chain construction: prompt-chain (canonical) and retrain-chain (LoRA rank 16)
- 15D hand-crafted features: surprisal stats (6), lexical stats (5), structural stats (4)
- K* measurement: LightGBM classifier, 5-fold CV AUC, bootstrap 95% CI (10K resamples), threshold τ=0.60
- Downstream filtering: 6 strategies evaluated on continued pretraining with lm-eval-harness (MMLU, HellaSwag), 5 seeds, paired t-tests
- Models: Qwen2.5-1.5B (main), Qwen2.5-7B, Pythia-1.4B
- Data: C4, 5K docs (1K for 7B), depths 0–4

**Experiment design reference** (what was planned):
10 experiments were designed. The paper covers all 10:
1. Decoding strategy ablation (exp-003): 4 strategies + baseline
2. Cross-scale probe 7B (exp-004): 1K docs, depth 0–3
3. Downstream filtering (exp-006): 6 strategies, 5 seeds, α sweep
4. Retrain-chain ecological validity (exp-008): Pythia-1.4B LoRA
5. Contamination dose-response (exp-009): 10/30/50% injection
6. LLM zero-shot baseline (exp-011): GPT-4o, Claude 3.5
7. Binary detector as depth proxy (exp-012): DivEye, Binoculars
8. Naturalistic validation (exp-014): Common Crawl 2025/2026
9. Learned representation baseline (exp-015): frozen embeddings + MLP
10. Base vs Instruct (exp-016): Qwen2.5-1.5B variants
11. Seed stability (worst-tier-auxiliary): 3 additional seeds

## Your Review Task

Evaluate the following dimensions in depth:

1. **Methodological soundness**: Is the DPI framework correctly applied? Is K* well-defined? Are there gaps in the theoretical-to-empirical bridge?

2. **Experimental design**: Are the experiments well-controlled? Are baselines appropriate? Is the evaluation protocol (τ=0.60, bootstrap CI) well-justified?

3. **Statistical rigor**: Are confidence intervals properly computed? Are the claimed significant/insignificant results supported? Is the seed stability analysis convincing?

4. **Completeness**: Are there obvious missing experiments or comparisons? Does the paper adequately ablate each factor?

5. **Reproducibility**: Are enough details provided to reproduce? Are there missing hyperparameters or implementation details?

6. **Weaknesses of the experimental design**: 
   - The 7B experiment uses only 1K docs (vs 5K for 1.5B) — how does this affect comparability?
   - Only 2 model families (Qwen, Pythia) — how generalizable?
   - Only 5 seeds for filtering — is this sufficient power?
   - The τ=0.60 threshold — is this choice well-motivated?
   - Hand-crafted 15D features are acknowledged as suboptimal, but most conclusions rest on them

7. **Internal consistency**: Do the results support each other? Are there contradictions?

## Output Format

Structure your review as:

(2-3 sentences)

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/paper_framing.md\", \"content\": \"# Paper Framing — gen-depth-contamination\\n\\n## Target Venue\\n- Primary: EMNLP 2026 Main (ARR submission May 25, 2026)\\n- Fallback: EMNLP 2026 Findings\\n- Backup: AAAI 2027 (abstract July 25, paper August 1)\\n\\n## Canonical Experimental Condition\\n\\n**Continuation mode 是 cross-model 结论的基础。**\\n\\n> \\\"We evaluate both generation modes because real-world recursive contamination can arise from either. Our analysis reveals generation mode as a significant confound, leading us to recommend continuation mode as the canonical condition for cross-model comparisons.\\\"\\n\\n| Condition | Pythia↔OLMo | Qwen↔others | 论文定位 |\\n|-----------|-------------|-------------|---------|\\n| Continuation | 91-92% retention | 80-85% retention | **主实验条件** — cross-model claims 的基础 |\\n| Rewrite (instruction) | N/A | 61-63% retention | **upper bound with mode-specific confound** — 仅作 ablation |\\n\\n**原则**：\\n- C1' 的跨模型 claim 必须基于 continuation mode 数据\\n- Rewrite mode 的高 AUC (0.6957 vs cont 0.6114) 是 mode-specific signal amplification，不能作为泛化性证据\\n- mvp-001 (Qwen-rewrite) 定位为 \\\"pilot study demonstrating feasibility\\\"，后续用 continuation mode rigorously validate\\n- cont→rw 89% vs rw→cont 82% 不对称性 → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\n\\n## Core Claims (2+1 结构, D012)\\n\\n### Primary: C1' (K* Characterization)\\n首次系统测量递归合成文本的 generational discrimination boundary K*。\\n\\nDPI 理论证明有限 K*_theory 必然存在（信息论 impossibility boundary）；我们首次在受控实验中测量 K*_empirical，它提供 K*_theory 的保守下界。通过 4-model × 2-mode 实验矩阵系统表征 K*_empirical 的行为，包括跨模型一致性、特征退化排序、以及 generation mode 对 depth signal 的调控效应。\\n\\n**子组件：**\\n\\n**(a) K* = 2 across all tested configurations**\\n> 4-model 1v2 bootstrap 95% CI 汇总：\\n> | Model | AUC 1v2 | 95% CI | CI lower > 0.60? |\\n> |-------|---------|--------|-----------------|\\n> | Qwen-rewrite | 0.6955 | [0.6859, 0.7041] | ✅ (+8.6pp) |\\n> | Pythia-1.4B | 0.6539 | [0.6454, 0.6630] | ✅ (+4.5pp) |\\n> | OLMo-1B | 0.6145 | [0.6045, 0.6247] | ✅ (+0.45pp) |\\n> | Qwen-cont | 0.6112 | [0.6015, 0.6213] | ✅ (+0.15pp, 最边缘) |\\n>\\n> - 全部 1v2 CI lower > 0.60, 全部 2v3 CI upper < 0.60, 全部 permutation p < 0.002\\n> - K* 不是离散跳变值，而是 CI-bounded continuum threshold\\n> - Paper 可写：\\\"K*=2 (bootstrap 95% CI for AUC₁ᵥ₂ > 0.60 across all four model configurations; AUC₂ᵥ₃ CI upper < 0.60)\\\"\\n\\n**(b) Cross-model transfer**\\n> - Pythia↔OLMo 91% accuracy retention → K*=2 不是 model self-familiarity artifact\\n> - 0v1 AUC > 0.96 across all transfer pairs → binary contamination signal 完全跨模型可迁移\\n> - 1v2+ transfer 取决于模型族相似度 → 与 K*=2 完美一致（K* 之上分辨力本就微弱）\\n> - depth signal ≈ 70-85% text-intrinsic + 15-30% model self-familiarity\\n\\n**(c) Degradation ordering consistency**\\n> - Spearman ρ ≥ 0.85 on single-feature AUC ranking（mode-controlled, continuation）\\n> - Top discriminative features shared: surp_d1_std, surp_std, surp_d2_std\\n> - Limitation: depth-pair level 一致性在 1v2 处较弱（τ~0.37）\\n> - Metric: single-feature AUC ranking（model-free, not LightGBM split count）\\n\\n**(d) Generation mode analysis (§4.x Analysis subsection)**\\n> - Signal strength: rewrite AUC 1v2=0.6957 vs continuation 0.6114（Δ=+8.4pp）\\n> - Transferability: continuation 80-92% retention vs rewrite 61-63%\\n> - Feature subset: cont→rw 89% > rw→cont 82% → continuation features 是 rewrite 的通用子集\\n> - Interpretation: rewrite mode 的 instruction-following 引入 mode-specific style artifacts，放大 depth signal 但降低跨模型泛化性\\n> - 论文定位：Analysis subsection（\\\"How does generation mode affect depth estimation?\\\"），不是独立 claim\\n\\n### Secondary: C3 (Graduated Filtering)\\n即使在 K*=2 的粗粒度 ordinal signal（human/gen-1/gen-2+ 三级）下，K*-aware graduated filtering 仍优于 binary filtering，因为 ordinal signal 在阈值附近提供更 calibrated 的质量估计。\\n\\n### Supporting Analysis: Canonical Condition Selection\\nGeneration mode 是 depth estimation 的显著混淆变量。Methodology 段落阐述 continuation mode 作为 canonical condition 的选择及理由（不作为独立 claim）。\\n\\n## Story Spine\\n\\n- **Problem**: Web 数据正被多代递归合成文本污染。当前检测方法将合成文本视为 binary 类别（real vs synthetic），但质量随代际深度非线性退化——首代合成文本与四代合成文本的质量差异巨大却被忽视。\\n- **Existing approaches & gaps**: Binary detectors (DivEye, Min-K%++) 不区分代际；model collapse 文献 (Shumailov et al., Alemohammad et al. 2024, Dohmatob et al. 2024) 描述宏观现象但不提供检测工具；data selection methods (DSIR, D4, DoReMi) 做分布匹配但不估计递归深度；Drayson et al. (2025) 将 binary MGT detection 应用于 importance resampling 以防止 model collapse，是目前最接近的工作。然而这些方法均做二值判断，**不估计文本经历了多少轮递归生成，也不量化辨别能力的饱和边界。**\\n- **Our framing**: 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，从信息论（DPI）推导出 K* 的必然存在。K* 是首次系统测量的 operational quantity——它告诉数据策展者\\\"在什么深度以下，depth-aware curation 比 binary detection 更有价值\\\"。通过 4-model × 2-mode 实验矩阵系统表征 K*，揭示控制因素（模型架构、解码策略、generation mode），为数据治理提供 actionable guidance。\\n- **Headline result template**: \\\"We establish K*=2 as the generational discrimination boundary for 1.5B-scale models under standard decoding, with depth features that are 70-85% text-intrinsic (Pythia↔OLMo 91% transfer retention). Even this coarse 3-class signal yields X% improvement over binary filtering on MMLU/HellaSwag.\\\"\\n\\n## Anticipated Reviewer Concerns & Responses\\n\\n1. **\\\"K*=2 太浅，graduated filtering 收益有限\\\"** → K* 是模型/解码策略依赖的；展示不同解码策略如何调控 K*（exp-003）；即使 K*=2，3-class 比 binary 在阈值附近更 calibrated（exp-006）\\n2. **\\\"只是 feature engineering + off-the-shelf classifier，方法贡献弱\\\"** → 贡献在 problem formulation + empirical characterization（首次测量 K*）+ 理论框架（DPI）+ generation mode analysis，不是分类器本身\\n3. **\\\"Prompt-chain 不代表真实世界递归污染\\\"** → retrain-chain ecological validity check 作为补充；prompt-chain 是 controlled experiment 的标准方法\\n4. **\\\"单模型/单数据集，泛化性不够\\\"** → 3 个模型族 × 2 种 generation mode + 4×4 cross-model transfer matrix\\n5. **\\\"同模型做生成和 surprisal 计算有 bias\\\"** → **exp-013 直接反驳**：Pythia↔OLMo 91% accuracy retention，0v1 transfer AUC > 0.96。Depth signal 主要编码在文本而非 model self-familiarity\\n6. **\\\"为什么不用 learned representations\\\"** → 作为 baseline 对比；hand-crafted features 更 interpretable 且支持 degradation ordering 分析\\n7. **\\\"Graduated filtering 没和 data selection methods 比\\\"** → exp-006 包含 DSIR baseline + α sensitivity sweep\\n8. **\\\"K* 的统计不确定性未量化\\\"** → 4-model bootstrap CI + permutation test（exp-007）\\n\\n## Venue Fit\\n\\n- EMNLP 2026 special theme 明确呼吁 data contamination, synthetic data feedback loops, data governance 方向的工作\\n- 2+1 claim 结构：C1' 是 comprehensive characterization study，C3 是 practical application，generation mode analysis 增加深度\\n- 风险：如果审稿人偏 methods，K*=2 + standard classifier 可能被认为贡献不足 → 强调 problem formulation + characterization + transfer analysis 的 novelty\\n\", \"total_chars\": 6221, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"}
```

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"path\": \"/home/ubuntu/.agent-ml-research-idea_gen_0509_7/projects/gen-depth-contamination/literature_map.yaml\", \"content\": \"papers:\\n  # === Must-Cite ===\\n\\n  - id: drayson2025\\n    title: \\\"Machine-generated text detection prevents language model collapse\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Drayson, Yilmaz, Lampos\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"EMNLP 2025\\\"\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2502.15654\\\"\\n    code: \\\"https://github.com/GeorgeDrayson/model_collapse\\\"\\n    category: \\\"verification-curation\\\"\\n    role: \\\"direct-competitor\\\"\\n    relevance: \\\"Binary MGT detection + importance resampling for collapse prevention; our depth estimator generalizes their binary detector to ordinal depth\\\"\\n    cited: false\\n    methodology: >\\n      Binary MGT detector (encoder-only transformer + classification head; best: ModernBERT-150M, AUC .986 in-dist, .943 OOD).\\n      Fine-tuned 5 epochs with label smoothing α=0.1 + temperature scaling.\\n      Sampling Importance Resampling (SIR): w_j = (1-q(x_j))^b / Σ(1-q(x_l))^b, where q(x) = P(machine-generated), b≥1 bias term.\\n      Resample at k=1.5 (50% upsampling). Evaluated on GPT-2 and SmolLM2 (124M–1.7B).\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"SIR filtering not only prevents collapse but improves over human-only training (perplexity -0.85% to -25.65%, diversity +3.44% to +14.62%, MAUVE +8.46% to +20.92%)\\\"\\n      - \\\"ModernBERT achieves best detection (AUC .986 in-dist); optimal threshold 0.8674 on imbalanced data (29% human)\\\"\\n      - \\\"Curated synthetic data has positive value — outperforms oracle (perfect filter) in equal-ratio settings\\\"\\n      - \\\"Only evaluates fine-tuning (not pretraining), models ≤2B, open-ended generation only\\\"\\n    positioning_notes: >\\n      Closest prior work. Their SIR is a special case of our graduated filtering when K*=1 (binary: human vs all synthetic).\\n      We generalize binary→ordinal: estimate depth d∈{0,1,...,K*} and apply depth-graduated weights.\\n      K* tells when ordinal signal adds value over binary. They never discuss ordinal depth or multi-class estimation.\\n      Our C3 claim directly extends their framework: \\\"even coarse 3-class signal yields better calibration at decision boundaries.\\\"\\n\\n  - id: schaeffer2025position\\n    title: \\\"Position: Model Collapse Does Not Mean What You Think\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Schaeffer, Kazdan, Arulandu, Koyejo\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"ICML 2025\\\"\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03150\\\"\\n    category: \\\"model-collapse\\\"\\n    role: \\\"framing-critical\\\"\\n    relevance: \\\"Identifies 8 definitions of model collapse across 3 families; forces us to position K* as discriminability saturation (distribution deformation), not test-loss collapse\\\"\\n    cited: false\\n    methodology: >\\n      Position paper. Surveys model collapse literature and identifies 8 distinct definitions organized in 3 families.\\n      Evaluates each definition against realistic deployment conditions. No experiments—purely analytical/argumentative.\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"8 definitions in 3 families: (A) Population risk degrading [D1: catastrophic increase, D2: any increase, D3: asymptotic divergence]; (B) Distributions deforming [D4: collapsing variance, D5: scaling law change, D6: mode disappearance/entanglement, D7: tail data loss]; (C) Spurious generation [D8: hallucinated data]\\\"\\n      - \\\"Under realistic conditions (data accumulation, growing compute), catastrophic risk divergence (D1-D3) is avoidable\\\"\\n      - \\\"Real harms—tail data loss (D7) and mode entanglement (D6)—have received disproportionately less attention\\\"\\n      - \\\"Recommends researchers explicitly state which definition they study\\\"\\n    positioning_notes: >\\n      K* belongs to Family B (distributions deforming), specifically D4 (collapsing variance) + D6 (mode entanglement).\\n      K* measures when distribution deformation renders generations indistinguishable in feature space.\\n      K* is NOT about population risk (Family A). This distinction is critical for framing:\\n      we contribute a diagnostic for exactly the harms Schaeffer et al. identify as underexplored.\\n      Adopt their taxonomy explicitly in Related Work to anchor K* precisely.\\n\\n  - id: kazdan2025collapse\\n    title: \\\"Collapse or Thrive? Perils and Promises of Synthetic Data in a Self-Generating World\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Kazdan, Schaeffer, Dey, Gerstgrasser, Rafailov, Donoho, Koyejo\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"ICML 2025\\\"\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2410.16713\\\"\\n    category: \\\"model-collapse\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    relevance: \\\"Accumulate vs replace regimes affect contamination dynamics; in accumulate regime K* may shift upward\\\"\\n    cited: false\\n    methodology: >\\n      Formal comparison of 3 data regimes: Replace (train on latest synthetic only; Σ→0 a.s.),\\n      Accumulate (train on all historical data; real fraction→0 but stability holds),\\n      Accumulate-subsample (fixed budget from accumulated pool; intermediate degradation).\\n      Theory: Gaussian setting with closed-form limits. Experiments: multivariate Gaussian, KDE, LLM SFT (Gemma2-2B on HelpSteer2/TinyStories).\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"Replace: covariance collapses to 0 (a.s.), W2 diverges. Collapse inevitable within 5-10 iterations\\\"\\n      - \\\"Accumulate: covariance stabilizes at σ₀²·sin(π/√n)/(π/√n). No collapse even as real data fraction→0\\\"\\n      - \\\"Accumulate-subsample: degradation plateaus rather than diverges\\\"\\n      - \\\"Cardinality of real data matters more than proportion (R²=0.59 vs 0.34)\\\"\\n    positioning_notes: >\\n      Our prompt-chain = replace regime (harshest setting). K*=2 measured under replace conditions.\\n      This makes K*=2 a conservative lower bound: under accumulate regime (more realistic), K* would shift upward.\\n      Strengthens our result: if depth estimation adds value at K*=2 under replace, it adds more under accumulate.\\n      Cite to justify experimental design and contextualize K* as regime-dependent.\\n\\n  - id: shi2025generalization\\n    title: \\\"A Closer Look at Model Collapse: From a Generalization-to-Memorization Perspective\\\"\\n    authors: \\\"Shi, Wu, Zhang, Zhang, Tao, Qu\\\"\\n    venue: \\\"NeurIPS 2025 Spotlight\\\"\\n    url: \\\"https://arxiv.org/abs/2509.16499\\\"\\n    code: \\\"https://shilianghe007.github.io/model-collapse/\\\"\\n    category: \\\"model-collapse\\\"\\n    role: \\\"background\\\"\\n    relevance: \\\"Generalization-to-memorization transition in iterative retraining parallels our feature-space convergence; entropy decrease over iterations aligns with our depth-discriminative features\\\"\\n    cited: false\\n    methodology: >\\n      Study iterative retraining of diffusion models (DDPM) on CIFAR-10 subsets (1K–32K images).\\n      Generalization Score (GS): avg distance between generated and nearest training image.\\n      Entropy: Kozachenko-Leonenko estimator. Strong correlation: Pearson r=0.91 between entropy and log(GS).\\n      Mitigation: entropy-based data selection (greedy max-NN-distance + threshold-decay filter).\\n    key_findings:\\n      - \\\"GS drops approximately exponentially across iterations; collapse within 5-10 iterations for large datasets\\\"\\n      - \\\"Training data entropy is a direct predictor of generalization capability (r=0.91)\\\"\\n      - \\\"Entropy-based data selection mitigates memorization transition\\\"\\n      - \\\"Diffusion models only; no LLM experiments. No theoretical analysis of WHY entropy collapses\\\"\\n      - \\\"Generated images form tight local clusters while overall variance remains relatively stable\\\"\\n    positioning_notes: >\\n      Parallel phenomenon across modalities: their entropy decline ↔ our JSD decrease (0.27→0.004).\\n      Their \\\"GS drops exponentially\\\" ↔ our feature-space divergence vanishes rapidly → K*=2.\\n      Key distinction: they measure output-distribution entropy; 35	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
```
```text
36	# Utility: simple 4-gram BLEU (no nltk dependency)
37	# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
38	
39	def ngrams(tokens, n):
40	    return [tuple(tokens[i : i + n]) for i in range(len(tokens) - n + 1)]
41	
42	
43	def simple_bleu(candidate_tokens, reference_tokens, max_n=4):
44	    """Compute sentence-level BLEU up to max_n grams with brevity penalty."""
45	    if len(candidate_tokens) == 0:
46	        return 0.0
47	    precisions = []
48	    for n in range(1, max_n + 1):
49	        cand_ngrams = ngrams(candidate_tokens, n)
50	        ref_ngrams = ngrams(reference_tokens, n)
51	        if not cand_ngrams:
52	            precisions.append(0.0)
53	            continue
54	        ref_counts = Counter(ref_ngrams)
55	        clipped = 0
56	        cand_counts = Counter(cand_ngrams)
57	        for ng, cnt in cand_counts.items():
58	            clipped += min(cnt, ref_counts.get(ng, 0))
59	        precisions.append(clipped / len(cand_ngrams))
60	    if any(p == 0 for p in precisions):
61	        return 0.0
62	    log_avg = sum(math.log(p) for p in precisions) / max_n
63	    bp = min(1.0, math.exp(1 - len(reference_tokens) / max(len(candidate_tokens), 1)))
64	    return bp * math.exp(log_avg)
65

71	def prepare_seed_data(tokenizer, data_dir: Path, num_samples: int, max_tokens: int = 256):
72	    """Download C4 validation, truncate to max_tokens, save as depth_0.jsonl."""
73	    out_path = data_dir / "depth_0.jsonl"
74	    if out_path.exists() and sum(1 for _ in open(out_path)) >= num_samples:
75	        log.info("depth_0.jsonl already exists with enough samples — skipping")
76	        return
77	
78	    from datasets import load_dataset

80	    # Use a single validation shard to avoid slow full-tree listing on proxied networks
81	    hf_endpoint = os.environ.get("HF_ENDPOINT", "https://huggingface.co")
82	    C4_SHARD_URL = f"{hf_endpoint}/datasets/allenai/c4/resolve/main/en/c4-validation.00000-of-00008.json.gz"
83	    log.info("Loading C4 validation shard (streaming) ...")
84	    try:

86	    except Exception as e:

88	        ds = load_dataset("allenai/c4", "en", split="validation", streaming=True)

92	        if i >= num_samples:
93	            break

114	REWRITE_PROMPT = (

126	def generate_depth_chain(
127	    model,
128	    tokenizer,
129	    data_dir: Path,
130	    max_depth: int,
131	    batch_size: int,
132	    max_new_tokens: int = 256,
133	    top_p: float = 0.95,

135	    do_sample: bool = True,
136	    generation_mode: str = "rewrite",
137	):
138	    """Generate depths 1..max_depth via prompt-chain rewriting."""
139	    for depth in range(1, max_depth + 1):
140	        out_path = data_dir / f"depth_{depth}.jsonl"
141	        prev_path = data_dir / f"depth_{depth - 1}.jsonl"
142	
143	        if out_path.exists() and sum(1 for _ in open(out_path)) >= sum(1 for _ in open(prev_path)):
144	            log.info(f"depth_{depth}.jsonl already complete — skipping")
145	            continue
146	
147	        prev_records = load_jsonl(prev_path)
148	        log.info(f"Generating depth {depth} ({len(prev_records)} docs, batch_size={batch_size}) ...")
149	
150	        results = []
151	        for start in tqdm(range(0, len(prev_records), batch_size), desc=f"depth {depth}"):
152	            batch = prev_records[start : start + batch_size]
153	
154	            if generation_mode == "rewrite":
155	                prompts = [REWRITE_PROMPT.format(prev_text=r["text"]) for r in batch]
156	
157	                inputs = tokenizer(
158	                    prompts,
159	                    return_tensors="pt",
160	                    padding=True,
161	                    truncation=True,
162	                    max_length=512,
163	                ).to(model.device)
164	
165	                with torch.no_grad():
166	                    outputs = model.generate(
167	                        **inputs,
168	                        max_new_tokens=max_new_tokens,
169	                        do_sample=do_sample,
170	                        top_p=top_p,
171	                        temperature=temperature,
172	                        pad_token_id=tokenizer.pad_token_id,
173	                    )
174	
175	                for j, out_ids in enumerate(outputs):
176	                    prompt_len = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]
177	                    generated_ids = out_ids[prompt_len:]
178	                    text = tokenizer.decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True).strip()
179	                    results.append({"text": text, "doc_id": batch[j]["doc_id"]})
180	
181	            else:  # continuation: batch generation with left-padding
182	                prefix_texts = []
183	                prefix_lengths = []
184	                for rec in batch:
185	                    full_ids = tokenizer.encode(rec["text"], add_special_tokens=False)
186	                    prefix_len = max(1, len(full_ids) // 2)
187	                    prefix_texts.append(tokenizer.decode(full_ids[:prefix_len], skip_special_tokens=True))
188	                    prefix_lengths.append(prefix_len)
189	
190	                inputs = tokenizer(
191	                    prefix_texts,
192	                    return_tensors="pt",
193	                    padding=True,
194	                    truncation=True,
195	                    max_length=256,
196	                ).to(model.device)
197	
198	                with torch.no_grad():

200	                        **inputs,

202	                        do_sample=do_sample,
203	                        top_p=top_p,
204	                        temperature=temperature,
205	                        pad_token_id=tokenizer.pad_token_id,
206	                    )
207	
208	                for j, out_ids in enumerate(outputs):
209	                    prompt_len = inputs["input_ids"].shape[1]
210	                    generated_ids = out_ids[prompt_len:]
211	                    text = tokenizer.decode(generated_ids, skip_special_tokens=True).strip()
212	                    results.append({"text": text, "doc_id": batch[j]["doc_id"]})
213	
214	            torch.cuda.empty_cache()
215	
216	        with open(out_path, "w") as f:
217	            for r in results:
218	                f.write(json.dumps(r, ensure_ascii=False) + "\n")
219	        log.info(f"Saved {len(results)} docs to {out_path}")

222	# ===================================================================
223	# Step 4: Feature extraction
224	# ===================================================================
225	
226	def compute_surprisal_features(model, tokenizer, texts, batch_size=8):
227	    """Compute 9-dim surprisal statistics per text using batched inference."""
228	    all_features = []
229	    for start in tqdm(range(0, len(texts), batch_size), desc="Surprisal"):
230	        batch_texts = texts[start : start + batch_size]
231	        inputs = tokenizer(
232	            batch_texts,
233	            return_tensors="pt",
234	            padding=True,
235	            truncation=True,
236	            max_length=512,
237	        ).to(model.device)
238	
239	        with torch.no_grad():
240	            logits = model(**inputs).logits  # (B, T, V)
241	
242	        log_probs = torch.log_softmax(logits, dim=-1)  # (B, T, V)
243	        input_ids = inputs["input_ids"]  # (B, T)
244	
245	        for i in range(len(batch_texts)):
246	            mask = inputs["attention_mask"][i].bool()
247	            seq_len = mask.sum().item()
248	            if seq_len < 3:
249	                all_features.append([0.0] * 9)
250	                continue
251	
252	            # left-padding: real tokens are right-aligned, use [-seq_len:]
253	            offset = input_ids.shape[1] - seq_len
254	            token_ids = input_ids[i, offset:]
255	            # surprisal of each token given prefix: -log p(x_t | x_{&lt;t})
256	            # shift: logits[t] predicts token[t+1]
257	            token_log_probs = []
258	            for t in range(1, seq_len):
259	                lp = log_probs[i, offset + t - 1, token_ids[t]].item()
260	                token_log_probs.append(-lp)  # surprisal = -log_prob
261	
262	            arr = np.array(token_log_probs)
263	            if len(arr) < 3:
264	                all_features.append([0.0] * 9)
265	                continue
266	
267	            d1 = np.diff(arr)
268	            d2 = np.diff(d1) if len(d1) > 1 else np.array([0.0])
269	
270	            feats = [
271	                float(np.mean(arr)),
272	                float(np.std(arr)),
273	                float(stats.skew(arr)),
274	                float(stats.kurtosis(arr)),
275	                float(np.mean(d1)),
276	                float(np.std(d1)),
277	                float(stats.skew(d1)) if len(d1) >= 3 else 0.0,
278	                float(np.mean(d2)),
279	                float(np.std(d2)),
280	            ]
281	            all_features.append(feats)
282	
283	        torch.cuda.empty_cache()
284	
285	    return all_features
286	
287	
288	def compute_vocab_diversity(texts, tokenizer, depth_texts_map, depth, sample_bleu=200, bleu_refs=100):
289	    """Compute 3-dim vocabulary diversity features: TTR, hapax ratio, self-BLEU."""
290	    features = []
291	    # tokenize all texts at word level (split on whitespace for simplicity)
292	    tokenized = [t.split() for t in texts]
293	
294	    # pre-select indices for self-BLEU (sample up to sample_bleu texts)
295	    bleu_indices = list(range(len(texts)))
296	    if len(bleu_indices) > sample_bleu:
297	        rng = np.random.RandomState(42 + depth)
298	        bleu_indices = rng.choice(bleu_indices, sample_bleu, replace=False).tolist()
299	    bleu_set = set(bleu_indices)
300	
301	    # pre-tokenize for BLEU refs
302	    same_depth_tokens = tokenized  # all texts from this depth
303	
304	    for idx, tokens in enumerate(tqdm(tokenized, desc=f"VocabDiv d={depth}", leave=False)):
305	        total = len(tokens)
306	        if total == 0:
307	            features.append([0.0, 0.0, 0.0])
308	            continue
309	
310	        unique = set(tokens)
311	        ttr = len(unique) / total
312	
313	        freq = Counter(tokens)
314	        hapax = sum(1 for v in freq.values() if v == 1)
315	        hapax_ratio = hapax / max(len(unique), 1)
316	
317	        # self-BLEU: compute for all samples (100 random refs each)
318	        rng_local = np.random.RandomState(idx)
319	        ref_pool = [j for j in range(len(same_depth_tokens)) if j != idx]
320	        if len(ref_pool) > bleu_refs:
321	            ref_pool = rng_local.choice(ref_pool, bleu_refs, replace=False).tolist()
322	        bleu_scores = [
323	            simple_bleu(tokens, same_depth_tokens[j]) for j in ref_pool
324	        ]
325	        self_bleu = float(np.mean(bleu_scores)) if bleu_scores else 0.0
326	
327	        features.append([ttr, hapax_ratio, self_bleu])
328	
329	    return features
330	
331	
332	def compute_tail_features(texts):
333	    """Compute 3-dim tail-thinning features: freq kurtosis, freq entropy, low-freq ratio."""
334	    features = []
335	    for text in texts:
336	        tokens = text.split()
337	        if len(tokens) < 2:
338	            features.append([0.0, 0.0, 0.0])
339	            continue
340	        freq = Counter(tokens)
341	        counts = np.array(list(freq.values()), dtype=float)
342	
343	        kurt = float(stats.kurtosis(counts)) if len(counts) >= 4 else 0.0
344	
345	        total = counts.sum()
346	        probs = counts / total
347	        entropy = float(-np.sum(probs * np.log2(probs + 1e-12)))
348	
349	        low_freq_ratio = float(np.sum(counts <= 2)) / max(len(counts), 1)
350	
351	        features.append([kurt, entropy, low_freq_ratio])
352	
353	    return features
354

356	SURPRISAL_COLS = [

358	    "surp_d1_mean", "surp_d1_std", "surp_d1_skew",
359	    "surp_d2_mean", "surp_d2_std",
360	]
361	VOCAB_COLS = ["ttr", "hapax_ratio", "self_bleu"]

423	def run_analysis(data_dir: Path, results_dir: Path, max_depth: int):
424	    """Pairwise AUC, JSD, K*, ordinal classification, feature importance."""
425	    import csv
426	
427	    import lightgbm as lgb
428	    from sklearn.model_selection import GroupKFold
429	    from sklearn.metrics import roc_auc_score, mean_absolute_error, accuracy_score
430	
431	    results_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
432	
433	    # load features
434	    feat_path = data_dir / "features.csv"
435	    log.info(f"Loading features from {feat_path} ...")
436	    with open(feat_path) as f:
437	        reader = csv.DictReader(f)
438	        rows = list(reader)
439	
440	    # build arrays
441	    depths = np.array([int(r["depth"]) for r in rows])
442	    doc_ids = np.array([int(r["doc_id"]) for r in rows])
443	    feat_matrix = np.array(
444	        [[float(r[c]) if r[c] != "" and r[c] != "nan" else np.nan for c in ALL_FEAT_COLS] for r in rows]
445	    )
446	    # fill NaN with column mean for classification
447	    col_means = np.nanmean(feat_matrix, axis=0)
448	    for j in range(feat_matrix.shape[1]):
449	        mask = np.isnan(feat_matrix[:, j])
450	        feat_matrix[mask, j] = col_means[j]
451	
452	    # --- Pairwise AUC ---
453	    log.info("Computing pairwise AUC ...")
454	    pairwise_auc = {}
455	    pairwise_auc_folds = {}
456	    for k in range(1, max_depth + 1):
457	        mask = (depths == k - 1) | (depths == k)
458	        X = feat_matrix[mask]
459	        y = (depths[mask] == k).astype(int)
460	        if len(np.unique(y)) < 2:
461	            pairwise_auc[f"{k-1}v{k}"] = float("nan")
462	            continue
463	
464	        gkf = GroupKFold(n_splits=5)
465	        groups = doc_ids[mask]
466	        fold_aucs = []
467	        for train_idx, test_idx in gkf.split(X, y, groups=groups):
468	            clf = lgb.LGBMClassifier(
469	                n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,
470	                num_leaves=31, verbose=-1, n_jobs=-1,
471	            )
472	            clf.fit(X[train_idx], y[train_idx])
473	            proba = clf.predict_proba(X[test_idx])[:, 1]
474	            fold_aucs.append(roc_auc_score(y[test_idx], proba))
475	        auc_mean = float(np.mean(fold_aucs))
476	        auc_std = float(np.std(fold_aucs))
477	        pairwise_auc[f"{k-1}v{k}"] = round(auc_mean, 4)
478	        pairwise_auc_folds[f"{k-1}v{k}"] = [round(a, 4) for a in fold_aucs]
479	        log.info(f"  AUC({k-1} vs {k}) = {auc_mean:.4f} ± {auc_std:.4f}  folds={[round(a,4) for a in fold_aucs]}")
480	
481	    with open(results_dir / "pairwise_auc.json", "w") as f:
482	        json.dump({"mean": pairwise_auc, "per_fold": pairwise_auc_folds}, f, indent=2)
483	
484	    # --- JSD ---
485	    log.info("Computing pairwise JSD ...")
486	    pairwise_jsd = {}
487	    for k in range(1, max_depth + 1):
488	        mask_prev = depths == k - 1
489	        mask_curr = depths == k
490	        feat_prev = feat_matrix[mask_prev]
491	        feat_curr = feat_matrix[mask_curr]
492	
493	        jsds_per_feat = []
494	        for j in range(feat_matrix.shape[1]):
495	            a = feat_prev[:, j]
496	            b = feat_curr[:, j]
497	            # histogram-based JSD
498	            lo = min(a.min(), b.min())
499	            hi = max(a.max(), b.max())
500	            if hi - lo < 1e-12:
501	                jsds_per_feat.append(0.0)
502	                continue
503	            bins = np.linspace(lo, hi, 51)
504	            p_hist = np.histogram(a, bins=bins, density=True)[0] + 1e-12
505	            q_hist = np.histogram(b, bins=bins, density=True)[0] + 1e-12
506	            p_hist /= p_hist.sum()
507	            q_hist /= q_hist.sum()
508	            m = 0.5 * (p_hist + q_hist)
509	            jsd = 0.5 * np.sum(p_hist * np.log2(p_hist / m)) + 0.5 * np.sum(q_hist * np.log2(q_hist / m))
510	            jsds_per_feat.append(float(jsd))
511	
512	        jsd_mean = float(np.mean(jsds_per_feat))
513	        pairwise_jsd[f"{k-1}v{k}"] = round(jsd_mean, 6)
514	        log.info(f"  JSD({k-1} vs {k}) = {jsd_mean:.6f}")
515	
516	    with open(results_dir / "jsd.json", "w") as f:
517	        json.dump(pairwise_jsd, f, indent=2)
518	
519	    # --- K* ---
520	    k_star = 0
521	    for k in range(1, max_depth + 1):
522	        key = f"{k-1}v{k}"
523	        if pairwise_auc.get(key, 0) > 0.60:
524	            k_star = k
525	
526	    # --- Ordinal classification ---
527	    log.info("Running ordinal LightGBM classification ...")
528	    gkf_ord = GroupKFold(n_splits=5)
529	    all_preds = np.zeros_like(depths, dtype=float)
530	    all_true = depths.copy()
531	
532	    importances = np.zeros(len(ALL_FEAT_COLS))
533	    for train_idx, test_idx in gkf_ord.split(feat_matrix, depths, groups=doc_ids):
534	        clf = lgb.LGBMClassifier(
535	            n_estimators=300, max_depth=8, learning_rate=0.05,
536	            num_leaves=63, verbose=-1, n_jobs=-1,
537	            num_class=max_depth + 1,
538	            objective="multiclass",
539	        )
540	        clf.fit(feat_matrix[train_idx], depths[train_idx])
541	        preds = clf.predict(feat_matrix[test_idx])
542	        all_preds[test_idx] = preds
543	        importances += clf.feature_importances_
544	
545	    importances /= 5.0
546	    mae = float(mean_absolute_error(all_true, all_preds))
547	    accuracy = float(accuracy_score(all_true, all_preds.astype(int)))
548	    log.info(f"  Ordinal MAE = {mae:.4f}, Accuracy = {accuracy:.4f}, K* = {k_star}")
549	
550	    ordinal_results = {"mae": round(mae, 4), "accuracy": round(accuracy, 4), "k_star": k_star}
551	    with open(results_dir / "ordinal_results.json", "w") as f:
552	        json.dump(ordinal_results, f, indent=2)
553	
554	    # --- Feature importance ---
555	    feat_imp = sorted(
556	        [{"feature": ALL_FEAT_COLS[i], "importance": float(importances[i])} for i in range(len(ALL_FEAT_COLS))],
557	        key=lambda x: x["importance"],
558	        reverse=True,
559	    )[:10]
560	    with open(results_dir / "feature_importance.json", "w") as f:
561	        json.dump(feat_imp, f, indent=2)
562	
563	    # --- Summary ---
564	    summary_lines = [
565	        "=" * 60,
566	        "MVP Pilot Saturation Study — Results Summary",
567	        "=" * 60,
568	        "",
569	        "Pairwise AUC (adjacent depths):",
570	    ]
571	    for k in range(1, max_depth + 1):
572	        key = f"{k-1}v{k}"
573	        folds = pairwise_auc_folds.get(key, [])
574	        std = float(np.std(folds)) if folds else 0.0
575	        summary_lines.append(f"  depth {k-1} vs {k}: AUC = {pairwise_auc.get(key, 'N/A')} ± {std:.4f}  folds={folds}")
576	    summary_lines += [
577	        "",
578	        "Pairwise JSD (adjacent depths):",
579	    ]
580	    for k in range(1, max_depth + 1):
581	        key = f"{k-1}v{k}"
582	        summary_lines.append(f"  depth {k-1} vs {k}: JSD = {pairwise_jsd.get(key, 'N/A')}")
583	    summary_lines += [
584	        "",
585	        f"K* (max depth with AUC > 0.60): {k_star}",
586	        "",
587	        f"Ordinal Classification (6-class, 5-fold CV):",
588	        f"  MAE = {mae:.4f}",
589	        f"  Accuracy = {accuracy:.4f}",
590	        "",
591	        "Top-10 Feature Importance:",
592	    ]
593	    for fi in feat_imp:
594	        summary_lines.append(f"  {fi['feature']:20s} {fi['importance']:.1f}")
595	    summary_lines.append("")
596	    summary_lines.append("=" * 60)
597	
598	    summary_text = "\n".join(summary_lines)
599	    with open(results_dir / "summary.txt", "w") as f:
600	        f.write(summary_text)
601	    log.info(f"\n{summary_text}")
602	
603

608	def main():

610	    parser.add_argument("--model_path", type=str,
```

```text
1	"""
2	exp-013: Cross-model transfer matrix for depth classifiers.
3	
4	Train OBD-LightGBM depth classifier on model A's features, test on model B's features.
5	Diagonal = within-model 5-fold CV; off-diagonal = full train→test transfer.
6	
7	Inputs:  4 features.csv files (one per model), each with 15 surprisal/lexical features + depth label.
8	Outputs: artifacts/cross_transfer_matrix.json, artifacts/cross_transfer_matrix.csv, printed 4×4 matrix.
9	"""
10	import argparse
11	import csv
12	import json
13	from pathlib import Path
14	
15	import numpy as np
16	from scipy.stats import kendalltau
17	from sklearn.metrics import mean_absolute_error, accuracy_score, roc_auc_score
18	from sklearn.model_selection import GroupKFold
19	import lightgbm as lgb
20	
21	FEAT_COLS = [
22	    "surp_mean", "surp_std", "surp_skew", "surp_kurt",
23	    "surp_d1_mean", "surp_d1_std", "surp_d1_skew",
24	    "surp_d2_mean", "surp_d2_std",
25	    "ttr", "hapax_ratio", "self_bleu",
26	    "freq_kurtosis", "freq_entropy", "low_freq_ratio",
27	]
28	
29	DEFAULT_DATA_DIRS = [
30	    "/root/autodl-tmp/gen-depth-contamination/data",
31	    "/root/autodl-tmp/gen-depth-contamination/data_exp001_pythia",
32	    "/root/autodl-tmp/gen-depth-contamination/data_exp002_olmo",
33	    "/root/autodl-tmp/gen-depth-contamination/data_exp017_qwen_cont",
34	]
35	DEFAULT_LABELS = ["Qwen-rewrite", "Pythia", "OLMo", "Qwen-cont"]
36	
37	MAX_DEPTH = 5
38	N_SPLITS = 5
39	SEED = 42
40	
41	LGB_PARAMS = dict(
42	    n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,
43	    num_leaves=31, verbose=-1, n_jobs=-1, random_state=SEED,
44	)
45	
46	
47	def load_features(path):
48	    with open(path) as f:
49	        reader = csv.DictReader(f)
50	        rows = list(reader)
51	    depths = np.array([int(r["depth"]) for r in rows])
52	    doc_ids = np.array([int(r["doc_id"]) for r in rows])
53	    X = np.array([
54	        [float(r[c]) if r[c] not in ("", "nan") else np.nan for c in FEAT_COLS]
55	        for r in rows
56	    ])
57	    col_means = np.nanmean(X, axis=0)
58	    for j in range(X.shape[1]):
59	        mask = np.isnan(X[:, j])
60	        X[mask, j] = col_means[j]
61	    return X, depths, doc_ids
62	
63	
64	def obd_cum_to_class_probs(cum_probs, n_classes):
65	    """Convert P(Y>k) cumulative probabilities to class probabilities."""
66	    probs = np.zeros((cum_probs.shape[0], n_classes))
67	    probs[:, 0] = 1.0 - cum_probs[:, 0]
68	    for k in range(1, n_classes - 1):
69	        probs[:, k] = cum_probs[:, k - 1] - cum_probs[:, k]
70	    probs[:, -1] = cum_probs[:, -1]
71	    probs = np.clip(probs, 0, 1)
72	    row_sums = probs.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)
73	    row_sums[row_sums == 0] = 1.0
74	    probs /= row_sums
75	    return probs
76	
77	
78	def train_obd(X_train, y_train):
79	    """Train K-1 OBD binary classifiers, return list of fitted models."""
80	    models = []
81	    for k in range(MAX_DEPTH):
82	        y_bin = (y_train > k).astype(int)
83	        clf = lgb.LGBMClassifier(**LGB_PARAMS)
84	        clf.fit(X_train, y_bin)
85	        models.append(clf)
86	    return models
87	
88	
89	def predict_obd(models, X_test):
90	    """Predict class probabilities and labels from OBD models."""
91	    n = X_test.shape[0]
92	    cum_probs = np.zeros((n, MAX_DEPTH))
93	    for k, clf in enumerate(models):
94	        cum_probs[:, k] = clf.predict_proba(X_test)[:, 1]
95	    class_probs = obd_cum_to_class_probs(cum_probs, MAX_DEPTH + 1)
96	    preds = class_probs.argmax(axis=1)
97	    return preds, class_probs
98	
99	
100	def within_model_cv(X, y, groups):
101	    """5-fold GroupKFold CV, return predictions and class probabilities."""
102	    n = len(y)
103	    all_preds = np.zeros(n, dtype=int)
104	    all_probs = np.zeros((n, MAX_DEPTH + 1))
105	    gkf = GroupKFold(n_splits=N_SPLITS)
106	    for train_idx, test_idx in gkf.split(X, y, groups=groups):
107	        models = train_obd(X[train_idx], y[train_idx])
108	        preds, probs = predict_obd(models, X[test_idx])
109	        all_preds[test_idx] = preds
110	        all_probs[test_idx] = probs
111	    return all_preds, all_probs
112	
113	
114	def cross_model_transfer(X_train, y_train, X_test):
115	    """Train on full source, predict on target."""
116	    models = train_obd(X_train, y_train)
117	    return predict_obd(models, X_test)
118	
119	
120	def pairwise_auc(y_true, y_probs):
121	    results = {}
122	    for k in range(1, MAX_DEPTH + 1):
123	        mask = (y_true == k - 1) | (y_true == k)
124	        if mask.sum() < 10:
125	            results[f"{k-1}v{k}"] = float("nan")
126	            continue
127	        y_bin = (y_true[mask] == k).astype(int)
128	        if len(np.unique(y_bin)) < 2:
129	            results[f"{k-1}v{k}"] = float("nan")
130	            continue
131	        score = y_probs[mask, k] if y_probs.ndim == 2 else np.zeros(mask.sum())
132	        try:
133	            results[f"{k-1}v{k}"] = round(float(roc_auc_score(y_bin, score)), 4)
134	        except ValueError:
135	            results[f"{k-1}v{k}"] = float("nan")
136	    return results
137	
138	
139	def evaluate(y_true, y_pred, y_probs):
140	    acc = round(float(accuracy_score(y_true, y_pred)), 4)
141	    mae = round(float(mean_absolute_error(y_true, y_pred)), 4)
142	    tau, tau_p = kendalltau(y_true, y_pred)
143	    pw = pairwise_auc(y_true, y_probs)
144	    mean_pw = round(float(np.nanmean(list(pw.values()))), 4)
145	    return {
146	        "accuracy": acc,
147	        "mae": mae,
148	        "kendall_tau_b": round(float(tau), 4),
149	        "kendall_tau_p": round(float(tau_p), 6),
150	        "pairwise_auc": pw,
151	        "mean_pairwise_auc": mean_pw,
152	    }
153	
154	
155	def main():
156	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="exp-013: Cross-model transfer matrix")
157	    parser.add_argument("--data_dirs", nargs="+", default=DEFAULT_DATA_DIRS,
158	                        help="Directories containing features.csv (one per model)")
159	    parser.add_argument("--labels", nargs="+", default=DEFAULT_LABELS,
160	                        help="Short labels for each model (same order as data_dirs)")
161	    parser.add_argument("--out_dir", default="/root/autodl-tmp/gen-depth-contamination/artifacts",
162	                        help="Output directory")
163	    parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=SEED)
164	    args = parser.parse_args()
165	
166	    assert len(args.data_dirs) == len(args.labels), \
167	        f"data_dirs ({len(args.data_dirs)}) and labels ({len(args.labels)}) must have same length"
168	
169	    np.random.seed(args.seed)
170	    out_dir = Path(args.out_dir)
171	    out_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
172	
173	    n = len(args.labels)
174	    datasets = {}
175	    for label, ddir in zip(args.labels, args.data_dirs):
176	        path = Path(ddir) / "features.csv"
177	        print(f"Loading {label}: {path}")
178	        X, y, groups = load_features(path)
179	        print(f"  {X.shape[0]} samples, depths {y.min()}-{y.max()}")
180	        datasets[label] = (X, y, groups)
181	
182	    matrix = {}
183	    for i, train_label in enumerate(args.labels):
184	        matrix[train_label] = {}
185	        for j, test_label in enumerate(args.labels):
186	            X_train, y_train, g_train = datasets[train_label]
187	            X_test, y_test, g_test = datasets[test_label]
188	
189	            if i == j:
190	                print(f"\n[{train_label} → {test_label}] within-model {N_SPLITS}-fold CV")
191	                preds, probs = within_model_cv(X_train, y_train, g_train)
192	            else:
193	                print(f"\n[{train_label} → {test_label}] cross-model transfer")
194	                preds, probs = cross_model_transfer(X_train, y_train, X_test)
195	
196	            metrics = evaluate(y_test, preds, probs)
197	            matrix[train_label][test_label] = metrics
198	            print(f"  acc={metrics['accuracy']:.4f}  MAE={metrics['mae']:.4f}  "
199	                  f"τ_b={metrics['kendall_tau_b']:.4f}  mAUC={metrics['mean_pairwise_auc']:.4f}")
200	
201	    # Print formatted matrix
202	    labels = args.labels
203	    print(f"\n{'='*80}")
204	    print("Transfer Matrix — Accuracy (MAE)")
205	    print(f"{'='*80}")
206	    header = f"{'Train \\ Test':<16}" + "".join(f"{l:>18}" for l in labels)
207	    print(header)
208	    print("-" * len(header))
209	    for tl in labels:
210	        row = f"{tl:<16}"
211	        for el in labels:
212	            m = matrix[tl][el]
213	            cell = f"{m['accuracy']:.3f} ({m['mae']:.3f})"
214	            row += f"{cell:>18}"
215	        print(row)
216	
217	    print(f"\nTransfer Matrix — Kendall τ_b")
218	    print("-" * len(header))
219	    header2 = f"{'Train \\ Test':<16}" + "".join(f"{l:>18}" for l in labels)
220	    print(header2)
221	    for tl in labels:
222	        row = f"{tl:<16}"
223	        for el in labels:
224	            m = matrix[tl][el]
225	            row += f"{m['kendall_tau_b']:>18.4f}"
226	        print(row)
227	
228	    # Compute transfer retention
229	    print(f"\n{'='*80}")
230	    print("Transfer Retention (off-diag / diagonal)")
231	    print(f"{'='*80}")
232	    for tl in labels:
233	        diag_acc = matrix[tl][tl]["accuracy"]
234	        for el in labels:
235	            if tl == el:
236	                continue
237	            off_acc = matrix[tl][el]["accuracy"]
238	            retention = off_acc / diag_acc if diag_acc > 0 else 0
239	            print(f"  {tl} → {el}: {off_acc:.4f} / {diag_acc:.4f} = {retention:.1%} retention")
240	
241	    # Save JSON
242	    json_path = out_dir / "cross_transfer_matrix.json"
243	    with open(json_path, "w") as f:
244	        json.dump({
245	            "matrix": matrix,
246	            "_meta": {
247	                "experiment": "exp-013",
248	                "description": "Cross-model transfer matrix for OBD depth classifiers",
249	                "models": labels,
250	                "method": "OBD-LightGBM",
251	                "n_splits_diagonal": N_SPLITS,
252	                "n_features": len(FEAT_COLS),
253	                "seed": args.seed,
254	            }
255	        }, f, indent=2)
256	    print(f"\nJSON saved: {json_path}")
257	
258	    # Save CSV
259	    csv_path = out_dir / "cross_transfer_matrix.csv"
260	    with open(csv_path, "w", newline="") as f:
261	        writer = csv.writer(f)
262	        writer.writerow(["train_model", "test_model", "accuracy", "mae",
263	                         "kendall_tau_b", "mean_pairwise_auc"])
264	        for tl in labels:
265	            for el in labels:
266	                m = matrix[tl][el]
267	                writer.writerow([tl, el, m["accuracy"], m["mae"],
268	                                 m["kendall_tau_b"], m["mean_pairwise_auc"]])
269	    print(f"CSV saved: {csv_path}")
270	
271	
272	if __name__ == "__main__":
273	    main()
```

```text
1	"""exp006 Filtering Strategies — 6 data quality filters for depth chain data.
2	
3	Input:  features.csv (30000 rows: doc_id, depth, 15 features)
4	        depth_*.jsonl (text data, needed for DSIR)
5	Output: per-sample weights [0,1] via get_weights(), boolean mask via get_mask()
6	
7	Strategies:
8	  1. no-filter      — uniform weights (baseline)
9	  2. binary-ours    — LightGBM binary (human vs synthetic), threshold=0.5
10	  3. binary-drayson — SIR resampling (Drayson et al. 2025), b=1.5, k=1.5
11	  4. graduated      — OBD-LightGBM ordinal + exp(-alpha*d) weighting
12	  5. surprisal      — surp_mean median threshold
13	  6. dsir           — n-gram importance resampling (Xie et al. 2023)
14	"""
15	
16	import argparse
17	import json
18	import os
19	from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
20	from collections import Counter
21	from hashlib import md5
22	
23	import numpy as np
24	import pandas as pd
25	
26	FEATURE_COLS = [
27	    "surp_mean", "surp_std", "surp_skew", "surp_kurt",
28	    "surp_d1_mean", "surp_d1_std", "surp_d1_skew",
29	    "surp_d2_mean", "surp_d2_std",
30	    "ttr", "hapax_ratio", "self_bleu",
31	    "freq_kurtosis", "freq_entropy", "low_freq_ratio",
32	]
33	
34	
35	class FilterStrategy(ABC):
36	    @abstractmethod
37	    def fit(self, features: np.ndarray, depths: np.ndarray, texts: list = None):
38	        ...
39	
40	    @abstractmethod
41	    def get_weights(self, features: np.ndarray, texts: list = None) -> np.ndarray:
42	        ...
43	
44	    def get_mask(self, features: np.ndarray, texts: list = None, threshold: float = 0.0) -> np.ndarray:
45	        return self.get_weights(features, texts) > threshold
46	
47	
48	class NoFilter(FilterStrategy):
49	    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):
50	        pass
51	
52	    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):
53	        return np.ones(len(features))
54	
55	
56	class BinaryOurs(FilterStrategy):
57	    """LightGBM binary: human (depth=0) vs synthetic (depth>=1).
58	    Weight = 1 - P(synthetic). Hard mask at P(synthetic) < threshold.
59	    Uses same 15D features as Graduated for fair comparison (C3 claim).
60	    """
61	
62	    def __init__(self, threshold=0.5):
63	        self.threshold = threshold
64	        self.model = None
65	
66	    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):
67	        import lightgbm as lgb
68	        labels = (depths > 0).astype(int)
69	        self.model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(
70	            n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,
71	            subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8,
72	            random_state=42, verbose=-1,
73	        )
74	        self.model.fit(features, labels)
75	
76	    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):
77	        return 1.0 - self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]
78	
79	    def get_mask(self, features, texts=None, threshold=None):
80	        t = threshold if threshold is not None else self.threshold
81	        proba_syn = self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]
82	        return proba_syn < t
83	
84	
85	class BinaryDrayson(FilterStrategy):
86	    """SIR (Drayson et al. 2025): w_j = (1-q(x_j))^b / Z, resample k*n."""
87	
88	    def __init__(self, b=1.5, k=1.5, seed=42):
89	        self.b = b
90	        self.k = k
91	        self.seed = seed
92	        self.model = None
93	
94	    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):
95	        import lightgbm as lgb
96	        labels = (depths > 0).astype(int)
97	        self.model = lgb.LGBMClassifier(
98	            n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,
99	            subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8,
100	            random_state=42, verbose=-1,
101	        )
102	        self.model.fit(features, labels)
103	
104	    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):
105	        q = self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]
106	        raw = (1.0 - q) ** self.b
107	        return raw / raw.max()
108	
109	    def get_mask(self, features, texts=None, threshold=None):
110	        q = self.model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]
111	        raw = (1.0 - q) ** self.b
112	        prob = raw / raw.sum()
113	        rng = np.random.RandomState(self.seed)
114	        n = len(features)
115	        selected = rng.choice(n, size=int(self.k * n), replace=True, p=prob)
116	        mask = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
117	        mask[np.unique(selected)] = True
118	        return mask
119	
120	
121	class Graduated(FilterStrategy):
122	    """OBD-LightGBM: K-1 binary classifiers for P(depth>k), then exp(-alpha * E[depth])."""
123	
124	    def __init__(self, alpha=0.5, n_depths=6):
125	        self.alpha = alpha
126	        self.n_depths = n_depths
127	        self.models = []
128	
129	    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):
130	        import lightgbm as lgb
131	        self.models = []
132	        for k in range(self.n_depths - 1):
133	            labels = (depths > k).astype(int)
134	            clf = lgb.LGBMClassifier(
135	                n_estimators=200, max_depth=6, learning_rate=0.05,
136	                subsample=0.8, colsample_bytree=0.8,
137	                random_state=42, verbose=-1,
138	            )
139	            clf.fit(features, labels)
140	            self.models.append(clf)
141	
142	    def predict_depth_distribution(self, features):
143	        """(n_samples, n_depths) matrix of P(depth=d)."""
144	        n = len(features)
145	        cum = np.zeros((n, self.n_depths - 1))
146	        for k, model in enumerate(self.models):
147	            cum[:, k] = model.predict_proba(features)[:, 1]
148	
149	        dist = np.zeros((n, self.n_depths))
150	        dist[:, 0] = 1.0 - cum[:, 0]
151	        for d in range(1, self.n_depths - 1):
152	            dist[:, d] = cum[:, d - 1] - cum[:, d]
153	        dist[:, -1] = cum[:, -1]
154	        dist = np.clip(dist, 0, 1)
155	        row_sums = dist.sum(axis=1, keepdims=True)
156	        row_sums[row_sums == 0] = 1
157	        dist /= row_sums
158	        return dist
159	
160	    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):
161	        dist = self.predict_depth_distribution(features)
162	        expected_depth = (dist * np.arange(self.n_depths)).sum(axis=1)
163	        weights = np.exp(-self.alpha * expected_depth)
164	        return weights / weights.max()
165	
166	
167	class SurprisalBased(FilterStrategy):
168	    """Keep samples with surp_mean >= median (high surprisal = more human-like)."""
169	
170	    def __init__(self, surp_col_idx=0):
171	        self.surp_col_idx = surp_col_idx
172	        self.threshold = None
173	
174	    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):
175	        self.threshold = np.median(features[:, self.surp_col_idx])
176	
177	    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):
178	        return np.where(features[:, self.surp_col_idx] >= self.threshold, 1.0, 0.0)
179	
180	
181	class DSIR(FilterStrategy):
182	    """Importance resampling via n-gram feature hashing (Xie et al. 2023).
183	    Reference distribution = depth-0 text, target = all text.
184	    """
185	
186	    def __init__(self, ngram=2, hash_dim=10000, k=1.5, seed=42):
187	        self.ngram = ngram
188	        self.hash_dim = hash_dim
189	        self.k = k
190	        self.seed = seed
191	        self.ref_dist = None
192	        self.target_dist = None
193	
194	    def _hash_features(self, text):
195	        tokens = text.lower().split()
196	        counts = Counter()
197	        for i in range(len(tokens) - self.ngram + 1):
198	            ng = " ".join(tokens[i:i + self.ngram])
199	            h = int(md5(ng.encode()).hexdigest(), 16) % self.hash_dim
200	            counts[h] += 1
201	        total = sum(counts.values()) or 1
202	        return {k: v / total for k, v in counts.items()}
203	
204	    def _build_dist(self, texts):
205	        agg = np.zeros(self.hash_dim)
206	        for text in texts:
207	            for k, v in self._hash_features(text).items():
208	                agg[k] += v
209	        agg += 1e-8
210	        agg /= agg.sum()
211	        return agg
212	
213	    def fit(self, features, depths, texts=None):
214	        assert texts is not None, "DSIR requires text data"
215	        ref_texts = [t for t, d in zip(texts, depths) if d == 0]
216	        self.ref_dist = self._build_dist(ref_texts)
217	        self.target_dist = self._build_dist(texts)
218	
219	    def _importance_weight(self, text):
220	        feats = self._hash_features(text)
221	        log_ratio = sum(
222	            v * (np.log(self.ref_dist[k]) - np.log(self.target_dist[k]))
223	            for k, v in feats.items()
224	        )
225	        return np.exp(log_ratio)
226	
227	    def get_weights(self, features, texts=None):
228	        assert texts is not None, "DSIR requires text data"
229	        weights = np.array([self._importance_weight(t) for t in texts])
230	        cap = np.percentile(weights, 99)
231	        weights = np.clip(weights, 0, cap)
232	        return weights / weights.max() if weights.max() > 0 else weights
233	
234	    def get_mask(self, features, texts=None, threshold=None):
235	        weights = self.get_weights(features, texts)
236	        prob = weights / weights.sum()
237	        rng = np.random.RandomState(self.seed)
238	        n = len(features)
239	        selected = rng.choice(n, size=int(self.k * n), replace=True, p=prob)
240	        mask = np.zeros(n, dtype=bool)
241	        mask[np.unique(selected)] = True
242	        return mask
243	
244	
245	STRATEGIES = {
246	    "no-filter": NoFilter,
247	    "binary-ours": BinaryOurs,
248	    "binary-drayson": BinaryDrayson,
249	    "graduated": Graduated,
250	    "surprisal": SurprisalBased,
251	    "dsir": DSIR,
252	}
253	
254	
255	def load_data(data_dir):
256	    """Load features.csv and depth_*.jsonl. Returns (df, texts, doc_ids)."""
257	    df = pd.read_csv(os.path.join(data_dir, "features.csv"))
258	    texts, doc_ids = [], []
259	    for depth in range(6):
260	        with open(os.path.join(data_dir, f"depth_{depth}.jsonl")) as f:
261	            for line in f:
262	                obj = json.loads(line)
263	                texts.append(obj["text"])
264	                doc_ids.append(obj["doc_id"])
265	    return df, texts, doc_ids
266	
267	
268	def main():
269	    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="exp006 filtering strategies")
270	    parser.add_argument("--data-dir", type=str, default="data/")
271	    parser.add_argument("--strategy", type=str, default="graduated",
272	                        choices=list(STRATEGIES.keys()))
273	    parser.add_argument("--alpha", type=float, default=0.5,
274	                        help="Decay param for graduated strategy")
275	    parser.add_argument("--output", type=str, default=None,
276	                        help="Save weights to .npy file")
277	    parser.add_argument("--seed", type=int, default=42)
278	    args = parser.parse_args()
279	
280	    np.random.seed(args.seed)
281	
282	    print(f"Loading data from {args.data_dir}")
283	    df, texts, _ = load_data(args.data_dir)
284	    features = df[FEATURE_COLS].values
285	    depths = df["depth"].values
286	
287	    kwargs = {}
288	    if args.strategy == "graduated":
289	        kwargs["alpha"] = args.alpha
290	    if args.strategy in ("binary-drayson", "dsir"):
291	        kwargs["seed"] = args.seed
292	    strategy = STRATEGIES[args.strategy](**kwargs)
293	
294	    print(f"Fitting: {args.strategy}")
295	    strategy.fit(features, depths, texts)
296	
297	    weights = strategy.get_weights(features, texts)
298	    mask = strategy.get_mask(features, texts)
299	
300	    print(f"Weights: mean={weights.mean():.4f} std={weights.std():.4f} "
301	          f"min={weights.min():.4f} max={weights.max():.4f}")
302	    print(f"Mask: {mask.sum()}/{len(mask)} kept ({mask.mean()*100:.1f}%)")
303	
304	    if args.output:
305	        np.save(args.output, weights)
306	        print(f"Saved to {args.output}")
307	
308	
309	if __name__ == "__main__":
310	    main()
311	
```

```text
1	# 信道级联 + DPI 理论框架草案
2	
3	> 将递归生成建模为 iterated lossy channel，用 Data Processing Inequality 约束 depth-discriminability 的 fundamental limit。
4	
5	---
6	
7	## 1. 信道级联模型形式化
8	
9	### 基本设定
10	
11	设 X₀ 为人类撰写的文本（seed text），表示为某概率空间上的随机变量。LLM 的生成过程建模为一个随机信道：
12	
13	$$\mathcal{T}: \mathcal{X} \to \mathcal{X}$$
14	
15	将输入文本映射到输出文本。递归生成形成 Markov chain：
16	
17	$$X_0 \to X_1 \to X_2 \to \cdots \to X_k$$
18	
19	其中 $X_{i+1} = \mathcal{T}(X_i)$，即 depth-$(i+1)$ 的文本由 depth-$i$ 的文本经过一次 LLM 信道产生。
20	
21	**关键假设**：
22	
23	- **(A1) 时不变信道**：同一 LLM + 同一 decoding strategy 在每次迭代中使用相同的信道 $\mathcal{T}$。（prompt-chain 设定下成立；retrain-chain 需放宽为 $\mathcal{T}_i$）
24	- **(A2) Markov 性**：$X_{i+1}$ 仅依赖 $X_i$，不依赖 $X_0, \ldots, X_{i-1}$（prompt-chain 逐段独立生成时成立）
25	- **(A3) 有损信道**：$\mathcal{T}$ 是 non-invertible 的——生成过程丢失关于输入的信息（sampling 引入 noise，decoding 策略截断分布尾部）
26	
27	### 形式化表述
28	
29	$$X_0 \xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}} X_1 \xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}} X_2 \xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}} \cdots \xrightarrow{\mathcal{T}} X_k$$
30	
31	这是一个齐次 Markov chain（在假设 A1 下），其转移核由 LLM 的条件分布 $p_\theta(\cdot | x)$ 决定。
32	
33	---
34	
35	## 2. Data Processing Inequality 约束
36	
37	### DPI 基本形式
38	
39	对于 Markov chain $X_0 \to X_1 \to \cdots \to X_k$，DPI 给出：
40	
41	$$I(X_0; X_k) \leq I(X_0; X_{k-1}) \leq \cdots \leq I(X_0; X_1) \leq H(X_0)$$
42	
43	即 $I(X_0; X_k)$ 关于 $k$ 单调非递增。
44	
45	**直觉**：每经过一次 LLM 信道，关于原始人类文本 $X_0$ 的信息只会减少或不变，绝不增加。递归生成是不可逆的信息丢失过程。
46	
47	### 严格衰减条件
48	
49	当 $\mathcal{T}$ 是 strictly lossy channel（即 $I(X_i; X_{i+1}) < H(X_i)$ for all $i$）时，衰减是严格的：
50	
51	$$I(X_0; X_k) < I(X_0; X_{k-1}) \quad \forall k \geq 1$$
52	
53	这对应于 LLM 生成过程中 sampling noise 不可消除的事实——任何 temperature > 0 或 nucleus/top-k 截断都引入不可逆信息损失。
54	
55	### 与 Depth Discriminability 的联系
56	
57	两个不同深度 $k, k'$ 的文本 $X_k, X_{k'}$ 的可区分性取决于它们的分布差异。定义可区分性度量：
58	
59	$$D(k, k') = d(P_{X_k}, P_{X_{k'}})$$
60	
61	其中 $d$ 是某种分布距离（JSD、MMD、Total Variation 等）。
62	
63	**关键观察**：随着 $k$ 增大，$P_{X_k}$ 趋近于信道 $\mathcal{T}$ 的不动点分布 $P^*$（如果存在），相邻深度的分布差异趋于零：
64	
65	$$D(k, k+1) \to 0 \quad \text{as} \quad k \to \infty$$
66	
67	---
68	
69	## 3. K* 的理论解释
70	
71	### 定义
72	
73	$K^*$ 是 **判别饱和边界（discriminability saturation boundary）**：
74	
75	$$K^* = \max\{k : D(k-1, k) > \epsilon\}$$
76	
77	其中 $\epsilon$ 是 noise floor（对应于有限样本下的统计检验 power limit）。
78	
79	### Information-Theoretic 解释
80	
81	$K^*$ 对应于 $I(X_0; X_k)$ 降至 noise floor 的临界深度：
82	
83	$$K^* \approx \min\{k : I(X_0; X_k) - I(X_0; X_{k+1}) < \delta\}$$
84	
85	其中 $\delta$ 是 MI 增量的可检测下限。
86	
87	**关键 insight**：$K^*$ 不是分类器的能力上限，而是 **information-theoretic impossibility boundary**——任何方法（无论多复杂）都无法区分 depth $> K^*$ 的文本，因为原始人类文本的信息已经被信道噪声完全淹没。
88	
89	### 与 Channel Capacity 的关系
90	
91	信道 $\mathcal{T}$ 的 "contraction rate" $\rho$ 控制 MI 衰减速度：
92	
93	$$I(X_0; X_k) \leq \rho^k \cdot H(X_0) \quad (\text{if exponential decay})$$
94	
95	- $\rho$ 接近 1（低噪声信道，如 greedy decoding）→ 衰减慢，$K^*$ 大
96	- $\rho$ 接近 0（高噪声信道，如 high-temperature sampling）→ 衰减快，$K^*$ 小
97	
98	**但这里有一个反直觉的 nuance**：虽然 high-temperature 使 $\rho$ 更小（信息丢失更快），但它同时保持了更高的 diversity，使相邻深度的分布差异可能更大。因此 $K^*$ 与 decoding strategy 的关系不是简单单调的——这正是需要实验验证的假设。
99	
100	---
101	
102	## 4. Propositions / Theorems 骨架
103	
104	### Proposition 1: MI 单调衰减（DPI 直接推论）
105	
106	**Statement**: 设 $X_0 \to X_1 \to \cdots \to X_k$ 为上述递归生成 Markov chain。则对任意 $k \geq 1$：
107	
108	$$I(X_0; X_k) \leq I(X_0; X_{k-1})$$
109	
110	等号成立当且仅当 $X_{k-1} \to X_k$ 是 sufficient statistic（即信道 $\mathcal{T}$ 是无损的，实际中不成立）。
111	
112	**Proof sketch**:
113	直接由 DPI 定理得出。$X_0 \to X_{k-1} \to X_k$ 构成 Markov chain，DPI 立即给出 $I(X_0; X_k) \leq I(X_0; X_{k-1})$。严格不等号由 $\mathcal{T}$ 的 strictly lossy 性质保证（sampling 引入不可消除的噪声）。$\square$
114	
115	**论文中的作用**：建立理论基础——depth discrimination 必然随深度退化，这不是分类器缺陷，而是信息论必然。
116	
117	---
118	
119	### Proposition 2: 不动点收敛与 K* 有限性
120	
121	**Statement**: 若信道 $\mathcal{T}$ 具有唯一不动点分布 $P^*$（即 $\mathcal{T}(P^*) = P^*$），且 $\mathcal{T}$ 关于某分布度量 $d$ 是 contractive 的（$d(\mathcal{T}(P), \mathcal{T}(Q)) \leq \rho \cdot d(P, Q)$，$0 < \rho < 1$），则：
122	
123	(a) $d(P_{X_k}, P^*) \leq \rho^k \cdot d(P_{X_0}, P^*)$
124	
125	(b) $D(k, k+1) = d(P_{X_k}, P_{X_{k+1}}) \leq (\rho^k + \rho^{k+1}) \cdot d(P_{X_0}, P^*)$
126	
127	(c) 对任意 $\epsilon > 0$，$K^*(\epsilon)$ 有限。
128	
129	**Proof sketch**:
130	(a) 由 contractive mapping theorem 的标准迭代 bound。
131	(b) 三角不等式：$d(P_{X_k}, P_{X_{k+1}}) \leq d(P_{X_k}, P^*) + d(P_{X_{k+1}}, P^*)$，代入 (a) 得结论。
132	(c) $D(k, k+1)$ 以几何速率趋于 0，因此超过 $\epsilon$ 的 $k$ 值数量有限。$\square$
133	
134	**论文中的作用**：证明 $K^*$ 的有限性是理论必然——saturation 不是方法缺陷而是系统属性。同时给出 $K^* = O(\log(d(P_{X_0}, P^*)/\epsilon) / \log(1/\rho))$ 的粗略 scaling。
135	
136	---
137	
138	### Proposition 3: 衰减速率与 Decoding Strategy 的关系（经验驱动假设）
139	
140	**Statement（Conjecture，标注为 empirically motivated）**: 设 $\rho(\tau)$ 为 temperature 为 $\tau$ 时信道 $\mathcal{T}_\tau$ 的 contraction rate。在以下简化假设下（unigram-level，忽略序列结构）：
141	
142	(a) $\rho(\tau)$ 关于 $\tau$ 非单调：存在 $\tau^*$ 使得 $\rho(\tau)$ 在 $\tau < \tau^*$ 时随 $\tau$ 递增，在 $\tau > \tau^*$ 时随 $\tau$ 递减。
143	
144	(b) 直觉：极低 temperature（近 greedy）→ 信道近确定性 → $\rho$ 大但 diversity 低 → MI 本身小。极高 temperature → 信道近均匀随机 → $\rho$ 小 → MI 快速衰减。中间 temperature 平衡两个效应。
145	
146	**Proof sketch**: 此为 conjecture，不提供证明。给出 unigram softmax 信道的显式 contraction rate 计算作为 supporting analysis，实验验证 $K^*(\tau)$ 的非单调性。
147	
148	**论文中的作用**：将 decoding strategy 对 $K^*$ 的影响从 ad hoc 观察提升为 hypothesis-driven 研究。用 AIC/BIC model comparison 区分 exponential vs. polynomial MI 衰减。
149	
150	---
151	
152	## 5. 计算上的 Tractable Proxy
153	
154	### JSD 作为 MI 近似
155	
156	Mutual information 在高维文本空间中不可直接计算。我们采用 Jensen-Shannon Divergence (JSD) 作为 computationally tractable proxy：
157	
158	$$\text{JSD}(P_{X_k} \| P_{X_{k'}}) = \frac{1}{2} D_{\text{KL}}(P_{X_k} \| M) + \frac{1}{2} D_{\text{KL}}(P_{X_{k'}} \| M)$$
159	
160	其中 $M = \frac{1}{2}(P_{X_k} + P_{X_{k'}})$。
161	
162	**在 unigram level 上计算**：将 $P_{X_k}$ 估计为 depth-$k$ 文档集合的 empirical unigram distribution。
163	
164	**局限性（必须在论文中承认）**：
165	- Unigram JSD 忽略序列结构（bigram, trigram, syntactic patterns）
166	- 不等价于真正的 MI——JSD 是 distributional divergence，MI 是 sample-level information quantity
167	- 但 DPI 的定性结论（单调衰减）仍成立，因为 $\text{JSD}(P_{X_k} \| P_{X_0})$ 在 Markov chain 下也满足类似的单调性
168	
169	**补充度量**：
170	- Pairwise classifier AUC 作为 empirical discriminability 的非参数度量
171	- Feature-space Mahalanobis distance 作为多维度的分布距离
172	
173	---
174	
175	## 6. 理论与实验的对接
176	
177	| 理论预测 | 实验验证方法 |
178	|---|---|
179	| $I(X_0; X_k)$ 单调衰减 | JSD$(P_{X_0}, P_{X_k})$ 随 $k$ 递减曲线 |
180	| $K^*$ 有限 | Pairwise AUC 矩阵 + 操作性 K* 定义 |
181	| 衰减速率 $\rho$ 由 decoding strategy 控制 | $K^*(\text{greedy})$ vs. $K^*(p{=}0.95)$ vs. $K^*(T{=}1.2)$ |
182	| Exponential vs. polynomial decay | AIC/BIC model comparison on JSD decay curves |
183	| 不动点 $P^*$ 存在 | 检验 $P_{X_k}$ 对 $k$ 的收敛性（JSD$(P_{X_k}, P_{X_{k+1}}) \to 0$） |
184	
185	---
186	
187	## 7. 与既有理论的关系
188	
189	- **Shumailov et al. (Nature 2024)**：他们的 tail disappearance → diversity loss → mode conflation 三阶段模型对应我们的 $P_{X_k} \to P^*$ 收敛过程中的不同阶段——尾部消失是收敛初期，mode conflation 是收敛后期
190	- **Strong Model Collapse (Dohmatob et al., ICLR 2025)**：他们的 statistical learning theory bound 与我们的 DPI bound 互补——他们关注 retrain-chain 下 generalization error 的增长，我们关注 distributional information 的衰减
191	- **JPEG 重压缩类比**：JPEG 信道也是 contractive mapping（量化步骤不可逆），K* 对应 JPEG recompression detection 的 cycle limit——在图像 forensics 中已被充分研究的问题
192	
193	---
194	
195	## 8. 写作注意事项
196	
197	1. **措辞校准**：Propositions 1-2 是严格的（DPI + contractive mapping 标准结果）；Proposition 3 标注为 "empirically motivated conjecture"，不 overclaim
198	2. **JSD proxy 局限性**：在 Method section 设专段讨论 unigram 近似的局限和 justification
199	3. **信道假设**：A1（时不变）和 A2（Markov）在 prompt-chain 下合理，在 retrain-chain 下需要放宽——在 Discussion 中讨论
200	4. **不做"完整证明"**：论文风格是 proposition + proof sketch + experimental validation，不是纯理论论文的 full proof 风格。重点在 theory-experiment connection
201	5. **Appendix 安排**：完整的 contraction rate 计算、unigram softmax channel 分析放 appendix
202	
```
